# SF-P2: How do evaluators embedded in a lab stay truly independent?

Area P · As of clef 7924 Berichte · Luna 7924 Berichte · JEV 5667 Berichte · amos 7924 Berichte · SubQ 7924 Berichte · drex 7924 Berichte · d3-ultra 5449 Berichte · d3-flash 1674 Berichte

## Subquestions

- [SF-P2-T01](../../fragen/SF-P2/T01/): Who selects, pays and may dismiss the evaluators, and is that contract published before the first finding? What minimum contract that preserves their independence could labs sign now, without waiting for legislation, including how reports are published? And how do the evaluators stay independent when money or infrastructure comes from the lab they audit?
- [SF-P2-T02](../../fragen/SF-P2/T02/): Before a verification arrangement between states builds on embedded evaluations, that is, on independent evaluators with ongoing, employee-like access inside AI companies, will the labs each commit that no embedded evaluation paid for by the lab being evaluated serves as evidence in that context? And will the governments treat lab-funded evaluation as insufficient per se for testing models for acute risks before release, so that the first embedded reports cannot quietly set a precedent?
- [SF-P2-T03](../../fragen/SF-P2/T03/): What access do the evaluators get: continuous, read-only access to training pipelines, not just to individual checkpoints, without creating new paths for data exfiltration? What minimum shared access standard will labs grant so that reports are comparable across the industry, who drafts it, and what would it take for one evaluator team to work at two competing labs at once? What controlled access to reasoning traces (a model's step-by-step reasoning) do they get, and what may they publish from them without enabling unauthorised distillation, that is, training another model on those traces?
- [SF-P2-T04](../../fragen/SF-P2/T04/): Are evaluators' access rights published, is there an independent, time-limited appeal against redactions, an independent process for disputes when a lab redacts a finding as commercially sensitive but the evaluator calls it a safety concern, and a public register of denied access and redactions, so that an evaluator's silence cannot be read as a sign of safety?
- [SF-P2-T05](../../fragen/SF-P2/T05/): What must an evaluator publish when a lab changes its safety framework, evaluation tooling or disclosure rules so that earlier commitments become less enforceable, for example by narrowing what counts as a reportable incident, and is the evaluator then protected from dismissal? And when evaluators continuously inspect AI agents doing autonomous research on model architectures and algorithms, which enforceable legal rules and measures against inadvertent knowledge transfer (a “cognitive quarantine”) ensure, during later audits and across staff turnover, that they do not carry proprietary algorithmic breakthroughs to competitors?
- [SF-P2-T06](../../fragen/SF-P2/T06/): When the head of a lab hints at an agreement between labs, which labs are part of it, and do independent evaluators with employee-like access belong to it? This was asked about Sam Altman (OpenAI), who in a Fortune interview of 12 September 2026 said a meeting with Dario Amodei (Anthropic), Elon Musk (xAI) and Demis Hassabis (Google DeepMind) was likely to happen.

## Reports by share of levels 4–7

Levels 4–7 / 3–7 = probability each model gives to these levels (3 = useful background or context, 4 = contains some facts that could be cited as evidence … 7 = directly and substantially answers the question). Listed: reports where clef or Luna give levels 3–7 more than 50 %. Sorted by the higher of clef and Luna for levels 4–7. Further models (JEV, amos, d3 …) rated part of the reports; the others show “not yet rated”. The full distribution over the 8 levels is in the data file.
